\zeta\to-\infty
, and is independent of
\mathsf{s}
and
\mathsf{x}
, the bound

|\Phi(\zeta)|+|\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\Phi(\zeta)|\leq f(\zeta)

is valid. From the explicit form of
\sigma_{\Phi}
in (
2.13
),

|\partial_{\mathsf{s}}\sigma_{\Phi}(\zeta)|=\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{s}-(-1)^{m}\zeta^{m}}}{\left(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{s}-(-1)^{m}\zeta^{m}}\right)^{2}}\leq\ee^{-\mathsf{s}-(-1)^{m}\zeta^{m}},\qquad\zeta<0.

Hence,
(|\Phi|+|\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\Phi|)\partial_{\mathsf{s}}\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}
is bounded by an
(\zeta,\mathsf{s})
-integrable function independent of
\mathsf{x}
, and we can indeed integrate (
3.52
) in the variable
\mathsf{s}
, obtaining the identity

(3.53)

\mathsf{p}(\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})=-\frac{4\alpha^{2}-1}{8\mathsf{x}}+\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{2\pi}\int_{\mathsf{s}}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\Phi(\xi\mid\mathsf{s}=u,\mathsf{x})^{2}\partial_{\mathsf{s}}\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}(\xi\mid\mathsf{s}=u)\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}u.

Differentiating now in
\mathsf{x}
, we obtain

\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\mathsf{p}(\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})=\frac{4\alpha^{2}-1}{8\mathsf{x}^{2}}+\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\pi}\int_{\mathsf{s}}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\Phi(\xi\mid\mathsf{s}=u,\mathsf{x})\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\Phi(\xi\mid\mathsf{s}=u,\mathsf{x})\partial_{\mathsf{s}}\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}(\xi\mid\mathsf{s}=u)\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}u.

Thus, combining this relation with (
3.37
), we finally conclude that

Theorem 3.12
.

For
(\zeta,\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,+\infty)
, the function
\Phi=\Phi(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})
satisfies the nonlocal PDE

(3.54)

\partial_{\mathsf{x}}^{2}\Phi(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})=\left(\zeta+\frac{4\alpha^{2}-1}{4\mathsf{x}^{2}}+\frac{2\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\pi}\int_{\mathsf{s}}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\Phi(\xi\mid u,\mathsf{x})\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\Phi(\xi\mid u,\mathsf{x})\partial_{\mathsf{s}}\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}(\xi\mid u)\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}u\right)\Phi(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x}).

For reference, we now obtain a formula that will later be used to relate the limiting kernel for the OP ensemble (
2.1
) with the function
\Phi
just constructed. Using the relation (
3.24
), we obtain the identity

\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\xi)})\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{-}}(\xi)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)})\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{-}}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21,-}=\\
\left[\mathbf{\Phi}_{\infty}(\mathsf{u}^{1/m}\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\infty}(\mathsf{u}^{1/m}\zeta)\right]_{21,-},

valid for
\zeta,\xi\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
. Using (
3.27
), (
3.33
), (
3.35
) and (
3.36
), we obtain

(3.55)

\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\xi)})\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{-}}(\xi)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)})\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{-}}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21,-}=\\
\mathrm{i}\ee^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\left[\Phi(4\xi/\mathsf{x}^{2})(\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\Phi)(4\zeta/\mathsf{x}^{2})-\Phi(4\zeta/\mathsf{x}^{2})(\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\Phi)(4\xi/\mathsf{x}^{2})\right],

where
\Phi
is the same function appearing in (
3.54
), and we used (
3.38
).

In particular, comparing with (
2.16
) we obtain the RHP representation of the kernel
\mathsf{K}_{\alpha}
,

(3.56)

\mathsf{K}_{\alpha}(\zeta,\xi)=\frac{\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\sqrt{\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}\left(-4\xi/\mathsf{x}^{2}\right)}\sqrt{\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}\left(-4\zeta/\mathsf{x}^{2}\right)}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}(\xi-\zeta)}\\
\times\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}\left(-4\xi/\mathsf{x}^{2}\right)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(-\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(-\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}\left(-4\zeta/\mathsf{x}^{2}\right)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21,-},

which is valid for
\xi,\zeta\in(0,\infty)
.

Recall the notation (
2.24
). Sending
\xi\to\zeta
, we also obtain the identity

(3.57)

\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}\left(4\zeta/\mathsf{x}^{2}\right)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]\right]_{21,-}=\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}\left(4\zeta/\mathsf{x}^{2}\right)}\mathsf{K}_{\alpha}(-\zeta,-\zeta),\quad\zeta<0.

These representations will be useful later. We now move on to computing relevant asymptotics for the model problem.

4.
The model RHP: admissible data and asymptotics

In the course of the analysis of OPs, the model problem needed will involve a function
\mathsf{h}=\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
which depends on a large parameter
\tau>0
. Later on, we will set this parameter
\tau
to be the number of particles
n
, but during this section we keep
\tau
as a free parameter. In the limit
\tau\to\infty
, the function
\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
will converge to
\mathsf{h}_{\infty}
from (
3.21
). The goal of the current section is to perform the required asymptotic analysis of the corresponding
\tau
-dependent model problem
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}=\mathbf{\Psi}(\cdot\mid\mathsf{h}=\mathsf{h}_{\tau})
, showing rigorously that it converges to the model problem
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\cdot\mid\mathsf{h}=\mathsf{h}_{\infty})
from (
3.22
).

The remainder of this section is structured as follows. In Section
4.1
we introduce the conditions on the function
\mathsf{h}=\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
that we will be working with. This function
\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
will also depend on the two additional parameters
\mathsf{s}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathsf{x}>0
. In Section
4.2
we analyze the model problem
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
in the degenerate limit
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
. This will be needed in order to integrate
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
in the variable
\mathsf{s}
, to cope with the limit of the deformation formula (
2.12
). This analysis in the limit
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
will also provide the major steps for the asymptotic analysis when
\mathsf{x}\to 0^{+}
; the latter will be completed in Section
4.3
. Finally, in Section
4.4
we complete the third asymptotic analysis necessary for the model problem, namely the one in the limit
\tau\to+\infty
.

4.1.
The class of admissible
\mathsf{h}

We now consider the model problem
\mathbf{\Psi}
associated to a function
\mathsf{h}
which depends on a (large) parameter
\tau
in a certain structured manner, as we introduce next. For the next statement, recall that the contour
\Gamma
was introduced in (
3.1
) and also Figure
1
.

Definition 4.1
.

A function
\mathsf{h}:\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}
is
admissible
if it is of the form

\mathsf{h}(\zeta)=\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathsf{s}+\tau^{2m}\mathsf{H}\left(\frac{\zeta}{\tau^{2}}\right),

where
\mathsf{s}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\tau>0
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
are parameters, and
\mathsf{H}:\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}
is a fixed function with the following properties.

(i)

The function
\mathsf{H}
is
C^{\infty}
in a neighborhood of
\Gamma
and real-valued along
\Gamma_{0}
.

(ii)

The function
\mathsf{H}
is analytic on a disk
D_{\delta}
with

\mathsf{H}(\zeta)=(-1)^{m}\mathsf{u}\zeta^{m}+\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{m+1}),\quad\zeta\to 0,

where
\mathsf{u}>0
.

(iii)

There exists
\Lambda>0
for which

\re\mathsf{H}(\zeta)\geq\Lambda|\zeta|^{m},\quad\zeta\in\Gamma.

For us we are interested in the choice of parameter
\tau=n
, with
n
as in (
2.1
), but in Definition
4.1
and for the rest of the present section we opt for introducing the new dummy parameter
\tau
, to emphasize that the model problem also has an interest on its own.

With
\mathrm{d}s
being the complex line element in
\Gamma
, Definition
4.1
–(iii) implies, in particular, that

|v|^{k}\ee^{-\mathsf{H}}\in L^{1}(\Gamma,|\mathrm{d}v|)\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma,|\mathrm{d}v|),\quad\text{for any }k>0.

Any admissible
\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
is analytic on a disk centered at the origin with growing radius. Moreover, the expansion

(4.1)

\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)=\mathsf{s}+(-1)^{m}\mathsf{u}\zeta^{m}(1+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-2}))

holds uniformly for
\zeta
in compacts of
\Gamma
, with an error term which is independent of
\mathsf{s}
. With
\mathsf{h}_{\infty}
as in (
3.21
), this shows in particular that

(4.2)

\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)=\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-2}),\quad\tau\to\infty,

again with the convergence being uniform in compacts of
\Gamma
, with an error term which is independent of
\mathsf{s}\in\mathbb{R}
. Observe, in particular, that the right-hand side coincides with the data studied in Section
3.3
. Since we are assuming
\mathsf{s}\in\mathbb{R}
, the convergence above also shows that
1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}}
is analytic and non-vanishing on a neighborhood of the origin, with this neighborhood being independent of
\tau
. Therefore, Proposition
3.2
applies for
\mathbf{\Psi}
with admissible
\mathsf{h}=\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
, for any
\tau
sufficiently large, a fact which will be used for the rest of the paper.

From now on, we fix
\mathsf{h}=\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
to always be an admissible function in the sense of Definition
4.1
and denote

(4.3)

\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{\Psi}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{h}_{\tau},\alpha),\quad\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{\Psi}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{h}_{\infty},\alpha),

with corresponding jump matrices

\mathbf{J}_{\tau}=\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}},\quad\mathbf{J}_{\infty}=\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}}.

This notation is consistent with (
3.22
). The convergence (
4.2
) indicates that
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}\to\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
. The main goal of the remainder of this section is to perform the asymptotic analysis to justify this convergence, and which should be valid uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
with any fixed
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.

4.2.
Asymptotic analysis for
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty

As said, we need to compare
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}
with
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
with bounds valid uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\in[\mathsf{s}_{0},\infty)
, where
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
fixed. In that analysis, we will also require bounds on appropriate norms of
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
valid uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
and also with some rough control as
\zeta\to\infty
along
\Gamma
. This section is devoted to obtain such bounds.

We start analyzing
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
when
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
. To that end, set

\mathbf{J}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{I}+\ee^{\pm\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\mathbf{E}_{21},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{\pm},\\
\mathbf{E}_{12}-\mathbf{E}_{21},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{0}.\end{cases}

That is,
\mathbf{J}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}
is the jump matrix for the model problem obtained with data
\ee^{-\mathsf{h}}\equiv 0
, corresponding to setting
\mathsf{h}=+\infty
. Let
\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha}
be the solution to the corresponding RHP, such solution is standard and can be constructed explicitly using Bessel functions, see Appendix
A
for more details.

The pointwise convergence

\mathbf{J}_{\infty}\to\mathbf{J}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}},\quad\mathsf{s}\to+\infty,

is straightforward, and leads us to compare
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
with
\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha}
. In fact, for
\alpha>-1/2
one could prove directly that the jump matrix for
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha})^{-1}
converges to the identity matrix in
L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}
as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
, which would suffice to apply small norm theory to estimate norms of
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
and of its boundary values. However, we could not improve such a direct argument also to the range
-1<\alpha\leq-1/2
. The alternate route still involves applying the steepest descent method, but the construction of a local parametrix is needed, as we discuss next.

Let
\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,0}
be the analytic function at the origin obtained when we apply Proposition
3.2
to
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
, and
\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}
the one when we apply the same proposition to
\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha}
. For a sufficiently small
\varepsilon>0
which we keep fixed from now on, the function
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,0}
is analytic on
D_{\varepsilon}
.

The function

(4.4)

\lambda(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left(\frac{1}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(s)}}-1\right)\frac{|s|^{\alpha}}{s-\zeta}\mathrm{d}s,\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{0},

is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{0}
, and satisfies

(4.5)

\lambda_{+}(\zeta)-\lambda_{-}(\zeta)=\left(\frac{1}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}}-1\right)|\zeta|^{\alpha},\quad\zeta<0.

Also, from basic properties of Cauchy transforms, we obtain the behavior

(4.6)

\lambda(\zeta)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{\alpha}),&\text{if }-1<\alpha<0,\\
\mathcal{O}(\log\zeta),&\text{if }\alpha=0,\\
\mathcal{O}(1),&\text{if }\alpha>0,\end{cases}\qquad\zeta\to 0.

We will use
\lambda
to construct the local parametrix, and some estimates on
\lambda
as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
will be needed. Using the inequality

\left|\frac{1}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}}-1\right|=\left|\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}}\right|\leq\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}=\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\ee^{-|\zeta|^{m}\mathsf{u}},\quad\zeta<0,

we obtain that

(4.7)

|\lambda(\zeta)|\leq\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\frac{|s|^{\alpha}\ee^{-\mathsf{u}|s|^{m}}}{|\zeta-s|}\mathrm{d}s,\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{0}.

Recalling that
\mathsf{u}>0
, we learn that
\lambda\to 0
uniformly for
\zeta
in compacts of
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{0}
as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
. Deforming contours in the integral defining
\lambda
, we see that this convergence also extends uniformly to the whole plane
\mathbb{C}
; more precisely

(4.8)

\|\lambda\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{0})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}),\quad\|\lambda_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{0})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}),\quad\mathsf{s}\to+\infty.

We use this function
\lambda
and set

(4.9)

\mathbf{P}_{0}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha,0}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}+\lambda(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)\mathbf{\zeta}^{\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}(\mathbf{I}+\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12})\\
\times(\mathbf{I}-(\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{+}}(\zeta)\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}-\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{-}}(\zeta)\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha})(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21}),\quad\zeta\in D_{\varepsilon}.

Next, we define

(4.10)

\mathbf{R}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)^{-1},&\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus(\Gamma\cup\overline{D}_{\varepsilon}),\\
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)\mathbf{P}_{0}(\zeta)^{-1},&\zeta\in D_{\varepsilon}\setminus\Gamma.\end{cases}

It is clear that
\mathbf{R}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus(\Gamma\cup\partial D_{\varepsilon})
. Using the jump properties of
\mathsf{a}
and
\lambda
given in (
3.8
) and (
4.5
), a cumbersome but straightforward calculation shows that

\mathbf{P}_{0,+}(\zeta)=\mathbf{P}_{0,-}(\zeta)\mathbf{J}_{\infty}(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in\Gamma\cap D_{\varepsilon},

that is,
\mathbf{P}_{0}
and
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
satisfy the same jumps for
\zeta\in\Gamma\cap D_{\varepsilon}
, implying in particular that
\mathbf{R}
has an isolated singularity at
\zeta=0
.

Also, using (
4.9
) and Proposition
3.2
with
\mathbf{\Psi}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
, it follows that

\mathbf{R}(\zeta)=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,0}(\zeta)(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)})^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\left(\mathbf{I}+\left[\zeta^{\alpha}\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\left(\frac{1}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}}-1\right)-\lambda(\zeta)\right]\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)^{-1},\quad|\zeta|<\varepsilon.

In virtue of (
4.6
), this shows that

(4.11)

\mathbf{R}(\zeta)=\mathit{o}(\zeta^{-1}),\;\zeta\to 0,\quad\text{as well as}\quad\mathbf{R}_{\pm}(\zeta)=\mathit{o}(\zeta^{-1}),\;\zeta\to 0\text{ along }\Gamma,

and therefore the singularity at
\zeta=0
is removable.

In summary, setting

\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left(\Gamma\setminus D_{\varepsilon}\right)\cup\partial D_{\varepsilon},

where
\partial D_{\varepsilon}
is oriented clockwise, we conclude that
\mathbf{R}
satisfies the following RHP.

RHP 4.2
.

Find a
2\times 2
matrix-valued function
\mathbf{R}:\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\to\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2}
with the following properties.

(i)

\mathbf{R}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}
.

(ii)

The entries of
\mathbf{R}_{\pm}
are continuous along
\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}
, and satisfy the jump relation
\mathbf{R}_{+}(\zeta)=\mathbf{R}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)
with

\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{I}+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}\ee^{\pm\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{21}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)^{-1},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{\pm}\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon},\\
\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{11}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)^{-1}+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{22}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)^{-1},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{0}\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon},\\
\mathbf{P}_{0}(\zeta)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)^{-1},&\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon},\end{cases}

and where we orient
\partial D_{\varepsilon}
in the clockwise direction.

(iii)

As
\zeta\to\infty
, we have

\mathbf{R}(\zeta)=\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{-1}).

(iv)

The entries of
\mathbf{R}_{\pm}-\mathbf{I}
belong to
L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Gamma,|\mathrm{d}s|)
.

Indeed, the properties (i)–(iii) are immediate from the corresponding properties of the RHPs satisfied by
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
and
\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha}
, and property (iv) follows from (
4.11
) and the continuity of
\mathbf{P}_{0},\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}},\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}
away from the origin.

To conclude the asymptotic analysis, we now analyze the behavior of
\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}
as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
. For the sake of clarity, we split this analysis into the next few lemmas. For their statements, we recall that the matrix norm notations were introduced in Section
2
.

Lemma 4.3
.

The estimate

\|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\partial D_{\varepsilon})}=\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\right)

holds as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
.

Proof.

Using the definition of
\mathbf{P}_{0}
and Proposition
3.2
with
\mathbf{\Psi}=\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha}
, we have that for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha,0}(\zeta)(\mathbf{I}+\lambda(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12})\zeta^{\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}(\mathbf{I}+\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12})

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \times\left[\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}\left(\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{+}}(\zeta)\ee^{\alpha\pi\mathrm{i}}-\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{-}}(\zeta)\ee^{-\alpha\pi\mathrm{i}}\right)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right]\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)\zeta^{-\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha,0}(\zeta)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha,0}(\zeta)\zeta^{\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\left[\lambda(\zeta)\zeta^{-\alpha}\mathbf{E}_{12}-\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}\left(\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{+}}(\zeta)\ee^{\alpha\pi\mathrm{i}}-\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{-}}(\zeta)\ee^{-\alpha\pi\mathrm{i}}\right)\right.

(4.12)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \left.\times(\mathbf{I}+(\mathsf{a}(\zeta)+\lambda(\zeta)\zeta^{-\alpha})\mathbf{E}_{12})\mathbf{E}_{21}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)\right]\zeta^{-\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha,0}(\zeta)^{-1}.

It is clear that
\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}})
uniformly in compacts of
\mathbb{C}
as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
. Also,
\mathsf{a}
is independent of
\mathsf{s}
. Next, we already observed the convergence
\lambda=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}})
as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
, uniformly in compacts (see (
4.8
)). The result then follows once we notice that
\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha,0}
and its inverse are both independent of
\mathsf{s}
and continuous functions of
\zeta
, and the contour
\partial D_{\varepsilon}
is obviously bounded.
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

The estimate

\|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{+}\cup\Gamma_{-}\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon})}=\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\right)

holds as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
.

Proof.

The asymptotic behavior in (
3.5
) for
\mathbf{\Psi}=\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha}
gives that as
\zeta\to\infty
along
\Gamma_{+}\cup\Gamma_{-}
,

\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{21}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)^{-1}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-4\zeta^{1/2}}\zeta^{1/2}),

with all the terms in the identity above being independent of
\mathsf{s}
. Along
\Gamma_{-}\cup\Gamma_{+}
, we have
\re\zeta^{1/2}=|\zeta|^{1/2}\cos\frac{\theta_{m}}{2}>0
, with
\theta_{m}
as in (
3.1
), and therefore the term on the right-hand side above has finite
L^{1}
and
L^{\infty}
norms along
\Gamma_{+}\cup\Gamma_{-}
. Likewise, the function
\ee^{(-1)^{m+1}\mathsf{u}\zeta^{m}}=\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)+\mathsf{s}}
has finite
L^{1}
and
L^{\infty}
norms along
\Gamma_{+}\cup\Gamma_{-}
, which are independent of
\mathsf{s}
as well. From the inequality

\|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{+}\cup\Gamma_{-})}\leq\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\|\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}+\mathsf{s}\pm\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}\mathbf{E}_{21}(\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}})^{-1}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{+}\cup\Gamma_{-})},

the lemma then follows.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

The estimate

\|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{0}\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon})}=\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\right)

holds as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
.

Proof.

Using again the asymptotic behavior in (
3.5
) for
\mathbf{\Psi}=\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha}
, we obtain that as
\zeta\to\infty
along
\Gamma_{0}
, and for
j=1,2

(4.13)

\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)+\mathsf{s}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{jj}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)^{-1}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{u}|\zeta|^{m}}\zeta^{1/2}),

with all the terms in the identity above being independent of
\mathsf{s}
. Using that
\mathsf{h}_{\infty}
is real along the real axis, we obtain the inequality
|1/(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}})|\leq 1
for
\zeta<0
. Proceeding now as in the proof of Lemma
4.4
, these estimates are sufficient to conclude the proof.
∎

We finally conclude

Proposition 4.6
.

The estimates

\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}),\quad\|\mathbf{R}_{\pm}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}})

are valid as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
.

Proof.

The estimates on
L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}
from Lemmas
4.3
–
4.5
extend to estimates valid on
L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}
as well, and the current lemma then follows from standard perturbation theory for RHPs.
∎

For later, we will need some consequences of the asymptotic analysis just carried out. We start stating a rough estimate for
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
, valid uniformly in
s
.

Proposition 4.7
.

Fix
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.
There exist constants
M>0
and
\eta>0
such that the inequalities

\left|\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{21}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\right|\leq M\ee^{-\eta|\zeta|^{1/2}},\quad\zeta\in(\Gamma_{+}\cup\Gamma_{-})\setminus D_{\varepsilon},

and

\left|\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{kk}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\right|\leq M|\zeta|^{1/2},\quad\zeta\in\Gamma_{0}\setminus D_{\varepsilon},\quad k=1,2,

are valid for any
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
.

Proof.

Since the jumps of
\mathbf{R}
are analytic, standard arguments on the asymptotic analysis of
\mathbf{R}
as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
ensure that

\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)=(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}))\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta),

uniformly along
\Gamma\setminus D_{\varepsilon}
as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
. The result now follows from the asymptotics (
3.5
) for
\mathbf{\Psi}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)
and the fact that
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}
is continuous along
\Gamma\setminus D_{\varepsilon}
and hence bounded on compact subsets of
\Gamma\setminus D_{\varepsilon}
.
∎

Next, we obtain estimates for quantities related to the integrable systems studied in Section
3.3
.
It is worth mentioning that the estimate in (ii) below is not optimal, but sufficient for our purposes.

Proposition 4.8
.

With the functions
\mathsf{p}=\mathsf{p}(\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})
and
\Phi(\zeta)=\Phi(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})
introduced in (
3.30
) and (
3.36
), respectively, we have the following estimates.

(i)

The estimate

\mathsf{p}(\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})=-\frac{4\alpha^{2}-1}{8\mathsf{x}}+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-s})

is valid as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
, uniformly for
\mathsf{x}>0
in compacts of
(0,+\infty)
.

(ii)

There exists
\delta>0
and
M>0
such that the estimate

|\Phi(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})|+|\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\Phi(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})|\\
\leq M\left(|\zeta|^{1/4}\chi_{(-\infty,-\delta)}(\zeta)+(\delta_{0}(\alpha)(\log|\zeta|)^{2}+|\zeta|^{-|\alpha|/2})\chi_{(-\delta,0)}(\zeta)\right),\quad\zeta<0,

is valid as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
, uniformly for
\mathsf{x}>0
in compacts of
(0,+\infty)
.

Proof.

The RHP
4.2
for
\mathbf{R}
is equivalent to the following integral equation:

\mathbf{R}(\zeta)=\mathbf{I}+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}}\mathbf{R}_{-}(s)(\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(s)-\mathbf{I})\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s-\zeta},\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}.

From this identity, condition (iii) in RHP
4.2
may be improved to

\mathbf{R}(\zeta)=\mathbf{I}+\frac{1}{\zeta}\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{-2}),\quad\zeta\to\infty,\quad\text{with}\quad\mathbf{R}_{1}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}}\mathbf{R}_{-}(s)(\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(s)-\mathbf{I})\mathrm{d}s.

Thanks to Proposition
4.6
,
\mathbf{R}_{1}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}})
as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
. On the other hand, from (
4.10
) and (
A.11
) we get the relation

(4.14)

\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,1}=\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,1}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\alpha).

Combining with (
3.30
) and (
A.14
), the result about
\mathsf{p}
follows.

The result about
\Phi
follows in a similar manner, as we now outline. The claimed estimate for
\zeta<0
and away from the origin follows from (
3.40
). From relations (
3.24
), (
3.36
) and (
3.27
), it follows that

\displaystyle\Phi(4\zeta/\mathsf{x}^{2})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\mathsf{x}}{2}\right)^{1/2}\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}+\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{+}}(\zeta)\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{11}

(4.15)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\left(\dfrac{\mathsf{x}}{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}\right)_{11}(\zeta),&\zeta>0,\\
\left(\dfrac{\mathsf{x}}{2}\right)^{1/2}\left[\left(\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}\right)_{11}(\zeta)+\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)})\left(\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}\right)_{12}(\zeta)\right],&\zeta<0.\end{cases}

For
-\varepsilon<\zeta<0
, we use (
4.9
) and (
4.10
), together with Proposition
4.6
, to express

\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,+}(\zeta)=\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}})\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)(\mathbf{I}+\lambda_{+}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12})\zeta^{\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}_{+}(\mathbf{I}+\mathsf{a}_{+}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12})(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21}).

The term
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}
is independent of
\mathsf{s}
and analytic in
\zeta
, hence bounded for
\zeta
in a neighborhood of the origin. From (
3.7
),
\mathsf{a}_{+}(\zeta)=\mathcal{O}(1+\delta_{0}(\alpha)\log|\zeta|)
. From (
4.6
), we also learn that
(\mathbf{I}+\lambda_{+}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12})\zeta^{\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}_{+}=\mathcal{O}(|\zeta|^{-|\alpha|/2}+\delta_{0}(\alpha)\log|\zeta|)
.
Finally, from (
3.21
), we see that
\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}})
as
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
, uniformly for
\zeta<0
. The result now follows from (
4.2
).
∎

4.3.
Asymptotic analysis of
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
as
\mathsf{u}\to+\infty

We also need to perform the asymptotic analysis of
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
as
\mathsf{u}\to+\infty
. The latter limit corresponds to
\mathsf{x}\to 0^{+}
, and this asymptotic analysis will provide (singular) boundary conditions for the functions
\mathsf{p}
and
\Phi
.

Our starting point in this asymptotic analysis will already be the function
\mathbf{R}
from (
4.10
), which is the solution to RHP
4.2
. The remaining challenge here is the analysis of the jump for
\mathbf{R}
as
\mathsf{u}\to+\infty
, as opposed to
\mathsf{s}\to+\infty
provided in the previous section. In other words, we need the analogues of Lemmas
4.3
–
4.5
, which we now provide.

Lemma 4.9
.

The estimate

\|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{I}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\partial D_{\varepsilon})}=\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{u}^{-(1+\alpha)/m}),\quad\mathsf{u}\to+\infty,

holds uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
with fixed
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

We analyze
\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}
along
\partial D_{\varepsilon}
through its explicit expression from (
4.2
), which for convenience of the reader we recall to be

\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha,0}(\zeta)\zeta^{\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\left[\lambda(\zeta)\zeta^{-\alpha}\mathbf{E}_{12}-\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}\left(\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{+}}(\zeta)\ee^{\alpha\pi\mathrm{i}}-\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{-}}(\zeta)\ee^{-\alpha\pi\mathrm{i}}\right)\right.\\
\left.\times(\mathbf{I}+(\mathsf{a}(\zeta)+\lambda(\zeta)\zeta^{-\alpha})\mathbf{E}_{12})\mathbf{E}_{21}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)\right]\zeta^{-\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha,0}(\zeta)^{-1}.

We next estimate the right-hand side above term by term.

The function
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}
and its matrix inverse
(\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}})^{-1}
are analytic in
\zeta
and independent of
\mathsf{u}
, hence remain uniformly bounded for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
. The inequality (
4.7
), together with a straightforward asymptotic analysis of the right-hand side through the classical saddle point method, unravels the rough estimate

\lambda(\zeta)=\mathcal{O}\left(\mathsf{u}^{-(1+\alpha)/m}\right),\quad\mathsf{u}\to+\infty,

valid uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
, and uniformly for
\zeta
in compacts of
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{0}
. A standard argument using deformation of contours shows that the same estimate is also valid down to the boundary values
\lambda_{\pm}(\zeta)
for
\zeta
along
\Gamma_{0}
, and therefore it is valid uniformly for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
. The factors
\zeta^{\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}
and
\mathsf{a}
are continuous functions of
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{0}
, with continuous boundary values along
\Gamma_{0}
, and independent of
\mathsf{u}
. Hence, they remain bounded for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
.

Finally, from the explicit form of
\mathsf{h}_{\infty}
from (
3.21
) and (
3.3
), we see that

|\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}|\leq\ee^{-\mathsf{s}-\mathsf{u}|\zeta|^{m}/2},\quad\zeta\in\mathcal{S}_{+}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-}.

Hence, for some
\eta>0
, the estimate
\ee^{-\mathsf{h}(\zeta)}\chi_{\mathcal{S}^{\pm}}(\zeta)=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\mathsf{u}})
holds uniformly for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
.

Plugging in all these estimates into (
4.2
), we obtain the lemma in the
L^{\infty}
norm, and the
L^{1}
claim follows from it and compactness of
\partial D_{\varepsilon}
.
∎

Remark 4.10
.

The proof of Lemma
4.9
actually shows a slightly stronger result, namely that

\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)=\mathbf{I}+\lambda(\zeta)\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha,0}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha,0}(\zeta)^{-1}+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\mathsf{u}}),\quad\mathsf{u}\to\infty,

uniformly for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
. Performing the change of variables
\mathsf{u}^{1/m}s=v
in (
4.4
), we obtain the asymptotic expansion

(4.16)

\lambda(\zeta)\sim\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda_{k}}{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{u}^{\frac{\alpha+1+k}{m}}\zeta^{k+1}},\quad\lambda_{k}=\lambda_{k}(\mathsf{s})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{(-1)^{k}}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\frac{|v|^{\alpha+k}\ee^{-\mathsf{s}-(-1)^{m}v^{m}}}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{s}-(-1)^{m}v^{m}}}\mathrm{d}v,\quad k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.

This, together with the analyticity of
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}
near the origin, allows us to compute an asymptotic expansion of
\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}
of the form

(4.17)

\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)\sim\mathbf{I}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\mathsf{u}^{\frac{\alpha+k}{m}}}\mathbf{J}_{k}(\zeta),\quad\mathsf{u}\to+\infty,\qquad\text{with}\quad\mathbf{J}_{k}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{\lambda_{k-1}}{\mathrm{i}\zeta^{k}}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha,0}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha,0}(\zeta)^{-1},

valid uniformly for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
. Notice that
\mathbf{J}_{k}
has a pole of order at most
k
at
\zeta=0
.

\triangleright

Lemma 4.11
.

Given
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists
\eta>0
such that the estimate

\|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}((\Gamma_{+}\cup\Gamma_{-})\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\mathsf{u}}),\quad\mathsf{u}\to+\infty,

is valid uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
.

Proof.

Proceeding as in the proof of Lemma
4.4
, we see that

\|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cup L^{\infty}((\Gamma_{+}\cap\Gamma_{-})\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon})}\leq\|\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}\mathbf{E}_{21}(\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}})^{-1}\|_{{L^{\infty}((\Gamma_{+}\cup\Gamma_{-})\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon})}}\|\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}}\|_{{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}((\Gamma_{+}\cup\Gamma_{-})\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon})}},

and the norm of
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}\mathbf{E}_{21}(\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}})^{-1}
above is finite. From (
3.3
), the norms of
\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}}
above are
\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\mathsf{u}})
, and the result follows.
∎

Lemma 4.12
.

Given
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists
\eta>0
such that the estimate

\|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{0}\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\mathsf{u}}),\quad\mathsf{u}\to+\infty,

is valid uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
.

Proof.

From the very definition of
\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}
in RHP
4.2
, it follows that, for
\zeta\in\Gamma_{0}\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon})
,

|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)-\mathbf{I}|\leq|\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}|\left(|\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{11}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)^{-1}|+|\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{22}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,-}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\zeta)^{-1}|\right).

The result now follows from (
4.13
).
∎

The asymptotic analysis is concluded with the next result.

Proposition 4.13
.

The estimates

\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}})}=\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{u}^{-(1+\alpha)/m})\quad\text{and}\quad\|\mathbf{R}_{\pm}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}})}=\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{u}^{-(1+\alpha)/m})

are valid as
\mathsf{u}\to+\infty
, uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
with fixed
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

With the help of Lemmas
4.9
,
4.11
and
4.12
, the conclusion is straightforward from perturbation theory for RHPs, and we skip it.
∎

We now draw the conclusions from this asymptotic analysis, namely asymptotic formulas for the function
\mathsf{p}
and
\Phi
from (
3.30
) and (
3.36
). For the next statement and its proof, recall that
\mathsf{x}=2\mathsf{u}^{-\frac{1}{2m}}
, see (
3.27
).

Proposition 4.14
.

With
\lambda_{0}
as in (
4.16
), the estimate

\mathsf{p}(\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})=-\frac{4\alpha^{2}-1}{8\mathsf{x}}+\frac{2\pi\lambda_{0}}{2^{2\alpha+1}\Gamma(\alpha+1)^{2}}\mathsf{x}^{2\alpha+1}+\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{x}^{2\alpha+3}),\quad\mathsf{x}\to 0^{+},

holds.

In addition, the asymptotic formula

(4.18)

\Phi(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})=\sqrt{\pi}\mathsf{x}^{1/2}I_{\alpha}(\mathsf{x}\zeta^{1/2})\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{x}^{2+2\alpha}(1+\mathsf{x}|\zeta|^{1/2}))\right),\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\},

is valid as
\mathsf{x}\to 0^{+}
, uniformly for
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
, and uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
with fixed
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.

Remark 4.15
.

Even though (
4.18
) is valid for
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
, it becomes useful only when
|\zeta|\ll\mathsf{x}^{-(4\alpha+2)}
, as in this case the
\mathcal{O}(\cdot)
term is truly a small error term. Nevertheless, this is a genuine asymptotic formula, in particular, for
\zeta
in compacts of
\mathbb{R}
.

\triangleright

Proof.

From (
3.30
), (
4.14
) and (
A.14
), it follows that

(4.19)

\mathsf{p}(\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})=-\frac{4\alpha^{2}-1}{8\mathsf{x}}+\frac{2\mathrm{i}}{\mathsf{x}}(\mathbf{R}_{1})_{12},

where we recall that

\mathbf{R}_{1}=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}}\mathbf{R}_{-}(s)(\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(s)-\mathbf{I})\mathrm{d}s=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\partial D_{\varepsilon}}\mathbf{R}_{-}(s)(\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(s)-\mathbf{I})\mathrm{d}s+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\mathsf{u}}),

and the last equality is a consequence of Proposition
4.13
and Lemmas
4.11
and
4.12
.
Writing
\mathbf{R}_{-}=(\mathbf{R}_{-}-\mathbf{I})+\mathbf{I}
, and appealing again to Proposition
4.13
and Remark
4.10
, we get

\mathbf{R}_{1}=-\frac{\mathsf{x}^{2\alpha+2}}{2^{2\alpha+3}\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\partial D_{\varepsilon}}\mathbf{J}_{1}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{x}^{4\alpha+4}),\quad\mathsf{x}\to 0^{+},

with
\mathbf{J}_{1}
as in (
4.17
). This coefficient has a simple pole at
\zeta=0
and no other poles, so a residue calculation provides

\int_{\partial D_{\varepsilon}}\mathbf{J}_{1}(s)\mathrm{d}s=-2\pi\lambda_{0}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(0)\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(0)^{-1},

with
\lambda_{0}
given in (
4.16
). We update (
4.19
) to

\displaystyle\mathsf{p}(\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\alpha^{2}-1}{8\mathsf{x}}+\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2^{2\alpha+1}}\mathsf{x}^{2\alpha+1}\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(0)\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(0)^{-1}\right]_{12}+\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{x}^{4\alpha+3})

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\alpha^{2}-1}{8\mathsf{x}}+\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2^{2\alpha+1}}\mathsf{x}^{2\alpha+1}\left(\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(0)\right]_{11}\right)^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{x}^{4\alpha+3})

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\alpha^{2}-1}{8\mathsf{x}}+\frac{2\pi\lambda_{0}}{2^{2\alpha+1}\Gamma(\alpha+1)^{2}}\mathsf{x}^{2\alpha+1}+\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{x}^{4\alpha+3}),\quad\mathsf{x}\to 0^{+},

where we have made use of (
A.9
) and (
A.10
) in the last equality.

Next, for the asymptotics of
\Phi
, we first use (
4.2
) and (
4.10
) to express

\Phi(\zeta)=\frac{\mathsf{x}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}}\times\begin{cases}\left[\mathbf{R}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)\right]_{11},&\zeta>\frac{4}{\mathsf{x}^{2}}\varepsilon\\
(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)^{\alpha/2}\left[\mathbf{R}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)\right]_{11},&0<\zeta<\frac{4}{\mathsf{x}^{2}}\varepsilon.\end{cases}

The term in the second line above depends solely on the first column of
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}
. In view of (
A.7
), (
A.8
) and (
A.10
), we are then able to re-express this identity in any of the two equivalent and uniform ways,

(4.20)

\Phi(\zeta)=\frac{\mathsf{x}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)^{\alpha/2}\left[\mathbf{R}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha,0}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)\right]_{11}=\frac{\mathsf{x}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\mathbf{R}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)\right]_{11},\quad\zeta>0.

Applying Proposition
4.13
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Phi(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathsf{x}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{x}^{2+2\alpha})\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)\right]_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathsf{x}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)\right)_{11}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{x}^{2+2\alpha})+\frac{[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)]_{21}}{[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)]_{11}}\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{x}^{2+2\alpha})\right),

valid as
\mathsf{x}\to 0^{+}
, uniformly for
\zeta>0
. Given a sufficiently large
M>
, we see from
A.13
that the quotient
(\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha})_{21}/(\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}_{\alpha})_{11}
is
\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{1/2}\mathsf{x})
for
\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta\geq M
. On the other hand, this same quotient is an entire function of
\zeta
(see (
A.1
), (
A.10
) and (
A.6
)), and therefore it is
\mathcal{O}(1)
for
0\leq\zeta\mathsf{x}^{2}\leq M
. In summary, we simplify this last asymptotic identity to

\displaystyle\Phi(\zeta)=\frac{\mathsf{x}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\mathbf{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\mathsf{x}^{2}\zeta/4)\right)_{11}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{x}^{2+2\alpha}(1+\mathsf{x}|\zeta|^{1/2}))\right),\quad\mathsf{x}\to 0^{+}.

The asymptotic claim for
\zeta>0
now follows from (
A.10
) and (
A.1
).

The claim for
\zeta<0
is very similar: a cumbersome but direct calculation shows that the first identity in (
4.20
) still holds for
\zeta<0
, and the remaining of the arguments remain pretty much the same.
∎

4.4.
Asymptotic analysis of the model RHP with admissible data

Recall that the matrix-valued function
\mathbf{\Psi}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}
was introduced in (
4.3
) as the solution to the model problem RHP
3.1
, with admissible data
\mathsf{h}=\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
as introduced in Definition
4.1
. As highlighted in (
4.2
)
et seq.
, we expect that
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}\to\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
as
\tau\to+\infty
, with the latter being the solution to the RHP with data
\mathsf{h}_{\infty}
given in (
3.21
), and the goal of this section is to show such convergence.

The asymptotic analysis is very similar to the one carried out in the previous section. The first step is the construction of a local parametrix
\mathbf{P}_{\tau}
in a fixed disk
D_{\varepsilon}
near the origin, which is the solution to the following RHP.

RHP 4.16
.

Find a
2\times 2
matrix-valued function
\mathbf{P}_{\tau}:D_{\varepsilon}\setminus\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2}
with the following properties.

(i)

\mathbf{P}_{\tau}
is analytic on
D_{\varepsilon}\setminus\Gamma
.

(ii)

On
\Gamma\cap D_{\varepsilon}\setminus\{0\}
,
\mathbf{P}_{\tau}
satisfies the jump relation

\mathbf{P}_{\tau,+}(\zeta)=\mathbf{P}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\mathbf{J}_{{}_{\tau}}(\zeta),

where we recall that

\mathbf{J}_{\tau}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}\mathbf{I}+(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\ee^{\pm\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\mathbf{E}_{21},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{\pm},\\
\dfrac{1}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\mathbf{E}_{12}-(1+\ee^{\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{0},\end{cases}

with
\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
as in Definition
4.1
.

(iii)

As
\tau\to\infty
,

\mathbf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)=(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-2}))\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta),

uniformly for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
, and also uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
with any fixed
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.

To construct the solution to this RHP, we first introduce the analogue of (
4.4
) to our context here, namely the function

(4.21)

\lambda_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left(\frac{1}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(s)}}-1\right)\frac{|s|^{\alpha}}{s-\zeta}\mathrm{d}s,\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{0}.

Using that
\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
is real-valued along the negative real axis, we obtain the inequality

(4.22)

\left|\frac{1}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}-1\right|\leq\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)},\quad\zeta<0.

Combined with the integrability condition asked in Definition
4.1
–(iii), we are ensured that
\lambda_{\tau}
is indeed well-defined for
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{0}
. In fact it is the Cauchy transform of a function in
L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{0})
, thus
\lambda_{\tau,\pm}
belongs to
L^{p}
for any
p\in(1,\infty)
. Since
\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
is analytic on a disk growing with
\tau
, we also see that for any compact
K\subset\Gamma_{0}
fixed, there exists
\tau_{0}=\tau_{0}(K)
for which
\lambda_{\tau,\pm}\in L^{\infty}(K)
for any
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
. This latter claim follows from deformation of contours in the definition of
\lambda_{\tau}
.

Fix a compact
K\subset\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}
. Using the inequality (
4.22
) and again the analyticity of
\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
in a
\tau
-independent neighborhood of the real axis, standard arguments show that there exists a constant
C_{K}>0
, depending solely on
K
, for which

|\lambda_{\tau}(\zeta)|\leq C_{K}\int_{-\infty}^{0}|s|^{\alpha}\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\mathrm{d}s,

for every
\zeta\in K
.

Similarly as for
\lambda
, the function
\lambda_{\tau}
satisfies the jump condition

(4.23)

\lambda_{\tau,+}(\zeta)-\lambda_{\tau,-}(\zeta)=\left(\frac{1}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}-1\right)|\zeta|^{\alpha}=-\frac{|\zeta|^{\alpha}\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}},\quad\zeta<0,

and the behavior

(4.24)

\lambda_{\tau}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{\alpha}),&-1<\alpha<0,\\
\mathcal{O}(\log\zeta),&\alpha=0,\\
\mathcal{O}(1),&\alpha>0,\end{cases}

as
\zeta\to 0
.

Using the function
\lambda_{\tau}
, we construct a solution to RHP
4.16
in the form

(4.25)

\mathbf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{E}_{\tau}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}+\lambda_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)\mathbf{\zeta}^{\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}(\mathbf{I}+\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12})\\
\times\left[\mathbf{I}-(\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{+}}(\zeta)\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}-\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{-}}(\zeta)\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha})(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right],\quad\zeta\in D_{\varepsilon}\setminus\Gamma,

where we recall that
\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{\pm}}
is the characteristic function of the set
\mathcal{S}_{\pm}
previously introduced in (
3.2
), the function
\mathsf{a}
is as in (
3.7
), and with the term
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,0}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}
being the matrix-valued analytic function obtained when we apply Proposition
3.2
to
\mathbf{\Psi}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
, we used the matrix prefactor

(4.26)

\mathbf{E}_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,0}(\zeta)\left(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}\right)^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\left(\mathbf{I}-\left(\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\zeta^{\alpha}}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}+\lambda_{\tau}(\zeta)\right)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right),\quad\zeta\in D_{\varepsilon}.

We now verify that (
4.25
) is indeed a solution to RHP
4.16
.

First of all, the factor
\mathbf{E}_{\tau}
is analytic near the origin. Indeed, all the terms involved in the definition of
\mathbf{E}_{\tau}
are analytic except across the negative axis. For
\zeta<0
, we compute

\mathbf{E}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{E}_{\tau,+}(\zeta)=\mathbf{I}-\left(\lambda_{\tau,+}(\zeta)-\lambda_{\tau,-}(\zeta)+\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\left(\mathsf{a}_{+}(\zeta)\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}-\mathsf{a}_{-}(\zeta)\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\right)|\zeta|^{\alpha}\right)\mathbf{E}_{12},

and using (
4.23
) and (
3.8
) the right-hand side above simplifies to
\mathbf{I}
. This shows that
\mathbf{E}_{\tau}
is analytic across the negative axis as well, so it has an isolated singularity at the origin. From the definition of
\mathbf{E}_{\tau}
, (
3.7
) and (
4.24
), we see that
\mathbf{E}_{\tau}(\zeta)=\mathit{o}(\zeta^{-1})
as
\zeta\to 0
, so this singularity is in fact removable.

We now come back to
\mathbf{P}_{\tau}
. Every factor in the right-hand side of (
4.25
) is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma
, so the same holds for
\mathbf{P}_{\tau}
, that is, RHP
4.16
–(i) is satisfied.

All the factors on the first line of the right-hand side of (
4.25
) are analytic across
\Gamma_{\pm}
, and the verification of RHP
4.16
–(ii) on
\Gamma_{\pm}
is immediate. After a cumbersome but straightforward calculation using (
4.23
) and (
3.7
), we verify that RHP
4.16
–(ii) is also satisfied along
\Gamma_{0}
.

Finally, we now verify RHP
4.16
–(iii). Using the definition of
\mathbf{E}_{\tau}
and Proposition
3.2
applied to
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
, we express for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
,

(4.27)

\mathbf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)^{-1}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,0}(\zeta)\zeta^{\frac{\alpha}{2}\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)})^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\\
\times\left[\mathbf{I}-\left(\frac{1}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}-\frac{1}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}}\right)\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right]\left(\mathbf{I}-\left(\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{+}}(\zeta)\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}-\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{-}}(\zeta)\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\right)(\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}-\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\\
\times(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)})^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}(\mathbf{I}-\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12})\zeta^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,0}(\zeta)^{-1}.

The term on the last line of (
4.27
) is the inverse of the term on the right-hand side of the first line, and these terms are independent of
\tau
. From the explicit expression for
\mathsf{h}_{\infty}
(recall (
4.2
)), it follows that we can choose
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small and independent of
\mathsf{s}\in\mathbb{R}
in such a way that such poles remain within a positive distance from
D_{\varepsilon}
. As such, the fraction
(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)})^{-1}
is bounded on
D_{\varepsilon}
uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
with any fixed
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. All in all, it means that the terms on the first and last lines of (
4.27
) are bounded functions of
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
, uniformly in
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
. Hence, to conclude RHP
4.16
–(iii) it suffices to prove that the terms on the second line of (
4.27
) are of the form
\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-2})
.

From the convergence in (
4.2
), we have

(4.28)

\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}-\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}=\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}(1-\ee^{\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)})=\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-2}\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}),

as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
\zeta
in compacts (and in particular for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
) and also uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
with any fixed
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. This estimate takes care of the second term in the second line of (
4.27
).

We already observed that
(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)})^{-1}
remains uniformly bounded for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
, and from the uniform convergence (
4.2
) we learn that
(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)})^{-1}
remains too uniformly bounded for
\zeta\in D_{\varepsilon}
and
\mathsf{s}\in\mathbb{R}
, and also uniformly as
\tau\to+\infty
. This way, we now bound

(4.29)

\left|\frac{1}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}-\frac{1}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}}\right|=\frac{|\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}-\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}|}{|1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}||1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}|}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-2}),\quad\tau\to\infty,

uniformly for
\zeta
in compacts (and again in particular for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
), where we used again (
4.28
). This is the required estimate for the first term in the second line of (
4.27
), and concludes the proof that
\mathbf{P}_{\tau}
from (
4.25
) solves RHP
4.16
–(iii).

To complete the asymptotic analysis, we use transform

(4.30)

\mathbf{R}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)^{-1},\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus(\Gamma\cup\overline{D}_{\varepsilon}),\\
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathbf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)^{-1},\quad\zeta\in D_{\varepsilon}\setminus\Gamma.\end{cases}

Recall that

\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gamma\cup\partial D_{\varepsilon}\setminus D_{\varepsilon},

we have that
\mathbf{R}
satisfies the following RHP.

RHP 4.17
.

Find a
2\times 2
matrix-valued function
\mathbf{R}:\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\to\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2}
with the following properties.

(i)

\mathbf{R}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}
.

(ii)

The entries of
\mathbf{R}_{\pm}
are continuous along
\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}
, and satisfy the jump relation
\mathbf{R}_{+}(\zeta)=\mathbf{R}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)
with

\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{I}+\left(\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}-\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}\right)\ee^{\pm\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{21}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)^{-1},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{\pm}\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon},\\
\mathbf{I}+\left(\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}-\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)\left(\frac{\mathbf{E}_{11}}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{22}}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)^{-1},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{0}\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon},\\
\mathbf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)^{-1},&\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon},\end{cases}

and where we orient
\partial D_{\varepsilon}
in the clockwise direction.

(iii)

As
\zeta\to\infty
, we have

\mathbf{R}(\zeta)=\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}\left(\zeta^{-1}\right).

Next, we estimate the jumps of
\mathbf{R}
to conclude the asymptotic analysis.

Lemma 4.18
.

Fix
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. For any
\kappa\in(0,2)
, the estimate

\|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-\kappa})

holds uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
as
\tau\to\infty
.

Proof.

For a given
\kappa\in(0,2)
, set
\nu\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(2-\kappa)/(m+1)\in(0,2/(m+1))
. We will make the estimates in three separate subsets of
\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}
, namely on
\partial D_{\varepsilon}
,
(\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\cap D_{\tau^{\nu}})\setminus\partial D_{\varepsilon}
and
\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\setminus D_{\tau^{\nu}}
.

From RHP
4.16
–(iii) we immediately obtain

(4.31)

\|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\partial D_{\varepsilon})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-2}).

Next, we move to the required estimates on
\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\cap D_{\tau^{\nu}}\setminus\partial D_{\varepsilon}
. We learn from (
4.2
) and Definition
4.1
–(ii) that there exists
M>0
, independent of
\mathsf{s},\tau
, for which

|\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)|\leq\frac{M}{\tau^{2-(m+1)\nu}}=\frac{M}{\tau^{\kappa}},\quad\zeta\in D_{\tau^{\nu}}.

In particular
\mathsf{h}_{\tau}-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}\to 0
uniformly for
\zeta\in D_{\tau^{\nu}}
as
\tau\to\infty
. From the inequality
|1-\ee^{w}|\leq|w|\ee^{|w|}
, valid for any
w\in\mathbb{C}
, and the fact that
|\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)+\mathsf{s}}|
is independent of
\mathsf{s}
and bounded by
\ee^{-\eta|\zeta|^{m}}
along
\Gamma
for some
\eta>0
, we learn

(4.32)

|\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}-\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}|\leq|\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}||\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)|\ee^{|\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)|}\leq\frac{M\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\ee^{-\eta|\zeta|^{m}}}{\tau^{\kappa}},\quad\zeta\in\Gamma\cap D_{\tau^{\nu}},

for a possibly different constant
M>0
, but still independent of
\mathsf{s},\tau
. Because
\mathsf{h}_{\tau},\mathsf{h}_{\infty}
are real-valued along
(-\infty,0)
, we have
|1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}}|^{-1},|1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}}|^{-1}\leq 1
. Then, using (
4.32
) and Proposition
4.7
, we conclude from
\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}
in RHP
4.17
–(ii) that

(4.33)

\|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}((\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\cap D_{\tau^{\nu}})\setminus\partial D_{\varepsilon})}\leq\frac{M\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}}{\tau^{\kappa}}\|\zeta^{1/2}\ee^{-\eta|\zeta|^{m}}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}((\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\cap D_{\tau^{\nu}})\setminus\partial D_{\varepsilon})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-\kappa}).

Finally, we move to obtaining the appropriate bounds on
\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\setminus D_{\tau^{\nu}}
. The very definition of
\mathsf{h}_{\infty}
in (
3.21
) already shows that for some
\eta_{1}>0
,

|\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}|\leq\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\ee^{-2\eta_{1}|\zeta|^{m}}\leq\ee^{-\mathsf{s}-\eta_{1}\tau^{m\nu}}\ee^{-\eta_{1}|\zeta|^{m}},\quad\zeta\in\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\setminus D_{\tau^{\nu}}.

Next, from Definition
4.1
–(iii) we learn that

|\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}|\leq\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\ee^{-\Lambda\tau^{\nu}/2}\ee^{-\Lambda|\zeta|^{\nu}/2},\quad\zeta\in\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\setminus D_{\tau^{\nu}}.

Combining these two inequalities, we obtain the estimate

(4.34)

|\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta)}-\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta)}|\leq M\ee^{-\mathsf{s}-\eta\tau^{\nu}-\eta|\zeta|^{\nu}},\quad\zeta\in\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\setminus D_{\tau^{\nu}}

for yet new constants
\eta>0,M>0
, both independent of
\mathsf{s}
. Therefore, from the definition of
\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}
and using again Proposition
4.7
, we obtain

(4.35)

\|\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\setminus D_{\tau^{\nu}})}\leq M\ee^{-\mathsf{s}-\eta\tau^{\nu}}\|\zeta^{1/2}\ee^{-\eta|\zeta|^{\nu}}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}\setminus D_{\tau^{\nu}})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}-\eta\tau^{\nu}}).

Estimates (
4.31
), (
4.33
) and (
4.35
) combined conclude the proof.
∎

Remark 4.19
.

For later reference, observe that (
4.32
) and (
4.34
) may be combined into the simpler estimate

\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})}-\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\eta|\zeta|^{\alpha}-\mathsf{s}}}{\tau^{\kappa}}\right),\quad\tau\to\infty,

for some
\alpha>0
and
\eta>0
, and valid uniformly for
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
.

\triangleright

We conclude the asymptotic analysis with the next statement.

Proposition 4.20
.

Fix
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. For any
\kappa\in(0,2)
the estimates

\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-\kappa}),\quad\|\mathbf{R}_{\pm}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-\kappa}),\quad\|\mathbf{R}_{\pm}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{2}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-\kappa})

are valid as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
.

Proof.

Once again, the result follows from standard perturbation theory of RHPs and the
L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}
estimates provided by Lemma
4.18
.
∎

Remark 4.21
.

For admissible
\mathbf{\Psi}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}
, the asymptotic expansion in RHP
3.1
–(iii) is valid as
\zeta\to\infty
in some neighborhood of
\infty
which in principle may depend on
\tau
. Nevertheless, thanks to Proposition
4.20
,
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}
may be well-modeled at
\zeta
by
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
, and consequently we may choose a uniform (in
\tau
) neighborhood of
\zeta=\infty
on which RHP
3.1
–(iii) is valid for every
\tau
sufficiently large.

\triangleright

Proposition
4.20
finishes the asymptotic analysis of
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}
, and in the next section we draw the main consequences that will be useful for later.

5.
Consequences of the asymptotic analysis for the model problem

We now summarize the consequences of the asymptotic analysis
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}\to\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
provided by Proposition
4.20
, and results surrounding it.

5.1.
Convergence of the kernel

Using Cauchy’s integral formula, we write for any
\xi,\zeta\in\mathbb{C}
,

\mathbf{R}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{R}(\zeta)=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{R}(\xi)^{-1}\left(\mathbf{R}(\zeta)-\mathbf{R}(\xi)\right)=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{R}(\xi)^{-1}\frac{\zeta-\xi}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\gamma}\frac{\mathbf{R}(u)}{(u-\zeta)(u-\xi)}\mathrm{d}u,

where
\gamma
is any contour encircling both
\zeta
and
\xi
in the counterclockwise direction. Applying Proposition
4.20
, we obtain

\mathbf{R}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{R}(\zeta)=\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-\kappa}(\zeta-\xi)),\quad\tau\to\infty,

uniformly in
\zeta,\xi
and
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
, for any
\kappa\in(0,2)
.

We unfold (
4.30
), concluding that

\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\zeta)=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\xi)^{-1}(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-\kappa}(\xi-\zeta)))\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta),\quad\tau\to\infty,

also uniformly in
\zeta,\xi
and
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
, for any
\kappa\in(0,2)
.
Since
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
is bounded on compact subsets of
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}
, and using (
4.29
), we conclude that

\frac{1}{\xi-\zeta}\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\tau}(\xi)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\tau}(\zeta)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21,+}=\\
\frac{1}{\xi-\zeta}\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\infty}(\xi)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21,-}+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-\kappa}),\quad\tau\to\infty,

valid uniformly in
\zeta,\xi
in compacts of
(-\infty,0)
and
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
, for any
\kappa\in(0,2)
, where recall that
\sigma_{\tau/\infty}(\xi)=(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau/\infty}(\zeta)})^{-1}
. A comparison with (
3.56
) using (
3.23
) reveals the next result.

Theorem 5.1
.

For any
\kappa\in(0,2)
, the estimate

\frac{1}{\zeta-\xi}\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\tau}(\xi)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\tau}(\xi)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21,-}=\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\mathsf{K}_{\alpha}(-\zeta,-\xi)}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\infty}(\xi)}\sqrt{\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)}}+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\tau^{-\kappa})

is valid as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
\zeta,\xi
in compacts of
(-\infty,0)
and uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq-\mathsf{s}_{0}
with any fixed
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.

5.2.
Asymptotics for a relevant integral

Fix
\zeta_{0}>0
and introduce

(5.1)

\mathsf{I}_{\tau}(\mathsf{s})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{\mathsf{s}}^{\infty}\int_{-\tau^{2}\zeta_{0}}^{0}\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid u)}}{(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid u)})^{2}}\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\tau}(\zeta)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]\right]_{21,-}\mathrm{d}\zeta\mathrm{d}u,

where we recall that
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}
is as explained in (
2.24
). This double integral will give the leading contribution to the multiplicative statistics (
2.5
).

If we apply Theorem
5.1
with
\xi\to\zeta
(which is possible due to the uniformity of the error term therein) and (
3.57
), we obtain as a consequence

\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]\right]_{21,-}=\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)}\mathsf{K}_{\alpha}(-\zeta,-\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})+\mathit{o}(1),\quad\tau\to\infty,

for
\zeta
in
(-\infty,0)
pointwise. But we want to have a more precise quantitative error estimate, valid also for
\zeta
near
0
to be able to perform the integration in (
5.1
) when
\tau\to\infty
, and for that we split into two cases, namely
\zeta<-\varepsilon
and
-\varepsilon<\zeta<0
.

For
\zeta<-\varepsilon
, a combination of (
4.30
) with Proposition
4.20
yields

\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})=\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}}{\tau^{\kappa}}\right)\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s}),\quad\tau\to\infty,

uniformly for
\zeta<-\varepsilon
and
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
with any
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, and where
\kappa\in(0,2)
is arbitrary. Using this estimate in combination with Remark
4.19
,
we obtain

\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)=\\
\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}}{\tau^{\kappa}}\right)\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\left(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{s}-\eta|\zeta|^{\alpha}}}{\tau^{\kappa}}\right)\right)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right),\quad\tau\to\infty,

with uniform error term as before. This identity may be differentiated term by term, and after a cumbersome but straightforward calculation it implies

\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]=\\
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}}{\tau^{\kappa}}\right)\mathbf{E}_{21}\left[\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(1)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right].

From (
3.5
) we get the rough estimate
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)=\mathcal{O}(\zeta_{-}^{1/4})
, valid for
\zeta\leq-\varepsilon
. Taking the
(2,1)
-entry and using (
3.57
) we update the above to

(5.2)

\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21,-}=\\
\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)}\mathsf{K}_{\alpha}(-\zeta,-\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\zeta_{-}^{1/4}}{\tau^{\kappa}}\right),\quad\tau\to\infty,

valid, as before, uniformly for
\zeta\leq-\varepsilon
,
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
and for any
\kappa\in(0,2)
.

Next, we consider
-\varepsilon\leq\zeta<0
. In this case, using (
4.25
), (
4.30
) and Proposition
4.20
, we obtain

(5.3)

\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)=\\
\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}}{\tau^{\kappa}}\right)\right)\mathbf{E}_{\tau}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}+\lambda_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\zeta^{\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}_{-}\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathsf{a}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right),\quad\tau\to\infty,

valid uniformly for
-\varepsilon\leq\zeta<0
and
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
, and for any
\kappa\in(0,2)
. For the record, we recall that
\mathbf{E}_{\tau}
is as in (
4.26
),
\lambda_{\tau}
is as in (
4.21
), and
\mathsf{a}
is as in (
3.7
).

Using the explicit expression for
\mathbf{E}_{\tau}
in (
4.26
) and Proposition
3.2
for
\mathbf{\Psi}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}
, we obtain the representation

\mathbf{E}_{\tau}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}+\lambda_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)\zeta^{-\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathsf{a}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}+(\ast)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right),

which is valid algebraically (not asymptotically!) for any
\zeta<0
, and where
(\ast)
represents a scalar that does not affect what comes next.

Returning this identity in (
5.3
), we obtain

(5.4)

\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)=\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}}{\tau^{\kappa}}\right)\right)\\
\times\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}+(\ast)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right),\quad\tau\to\infty,

valid uniformly for
-\varepsilon\leq\zeta<0
and
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
as before.

The matrix term of the form
\mathbf{I}+(\ast)\mathbf{E}_{21}
on the right-most side does not affect the final result of applying
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}
and taking the
(2,1)
-entry in this identity. Thus, from this identity and again Theorem
5.1
we obtain

\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]\right]_{21,-}=\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)}\mathsf{K}_{\alpha}(-\zeta,-\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})\\
+\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}}{\tau^{\kappa}}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21,-},\quad\tau\to\infty.

Again thanks to Proposition
3.2
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\infty,0}(\zeta)\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\zeta^{\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(1\right)\zeta^{\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathsf{a}(\zeta)\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right).

Using this estimate, the previous estimate simplifies to

(5.5)

\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})\left(\mathbf{I}-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\tau}(\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]\right]_{21,-}=\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)}\mathsf{K}_{\alpha}(-\zeta,-\zeta\mid\mathsf{s})+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}\zeta^{\alpha}}{\tau^{\kappa}}\right),

valid as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
-\varepsilon\leq\zeta<0
, and uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
with any
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, and where
\kappa\in(0,2)
is arbitrary.

Finally, we now insert (
5.2
) and (
5.5
) in (
5.1
) and use also the uniform decay of
\mathsf{h}_{\tau}
as
\zeta\to-\infty
which is ensured by Definition
4.1
–(iii), the convergence from Remark
4.21
,
and arrive at the following result.

Theorem 5.2
.

For any
\kappa\in(0,2)
and any
\mathsf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, the estimate

\mathsf{I}_{\tau}(s)=2\pi\mathrm{i}\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\int_{\mathsf{s}}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\mathsf{K}_{\alpha}(-\zeta,-\zeta\mid u)\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta\mid u)}}{1+\ee^{-\mathsf{h}_{\infty}(\zeta\mid u)}}\mathrm{d}\zeta\mathrm{d}u+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\mathsf{s}}}{\tau^{\kappa}}\right),\quad\tau\to\infty,

holds uniformly for
\mathsf{s}\geq\mathsf{s}_{0}
.

This result finishes the asymptotic analysis necessary on the model problem.

5.3.
Proof of main theorems on integrable equations

In this section we complete the proof of our main results that connect the limiting probabilistic quantities to integrable equations. They are essentially a recollection of several calculations that we already performed.

Proof of Theorem
2.3
.

Define
\Phi
as in (
3.36
). The nonlocal equation (
2.14
) for it was already obtained in Theorem
3.12
. The asymptotic behavior of
\Phi
as
\zeta\to\pm\infty
are given in (
3.39
) and (
3.40
). The asymptotic behavior as
\mathsf{x}\to 0^{+}
, in turn, was obtained in Proposition
4.14
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.7
.

Define
\mathsf{p}
as in (
3.30
). Then, identity (
3.53
) shows that this definition coincides with the one given in (
2.20
), and Proposition
4.14
yields its asymptotic behavior as
\mathsf{x}\to 0^{+}
as claimed by Theorem
2.7
.

It remains to show the connection with
\mathsf{L}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}
. For that, we start from (
3.57
) and use (
3.24
) and (
3.33
) to express

\mathsf{K}_{\alpha}(-\zeta,-\zeta\mid\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})=\frac{\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\mathsf{u}^{-1/m}\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\left[\mathbf{\Upsilon}(\mathsf{u}^{1/m}\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{\Upsilon}^{\prime}(\mathsf{u}^{1/m}\zeta)\right]_{21,-}.

Performing the change of variables
\xi=-\mathsf{u}^{1/m}\zeta=-\frac{4}{\mathsf{x}^{2}}\zeta
in (
2.19
), we obtain from (
3.23
) that

(5.6)

-\log\mathsf{L}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})=\frac{\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathsf{s}}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left[\mathbf{\Upsilon}(\xi\mid\mathsf{s}=u,\mathsf{x})^{-1}\mathbf{\Upsilon}^{\prime}(\xi\mid\mathsf{s}=u,\mathsf{x})\right]_{21,-}\partial_{\mathsf{s}}\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}(\xi\mid\mathsf{s}=u)\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}u.

Observe that
\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}
is independent of
\mathsf{x}
, recall (
2.13
). A direct calculation from the jump of
\mathbf{\Upsilon}
(which coincides with the jump of
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\infty}
in (
3.25
)) shows that
[\mathbf{\Upsilon}^{-1}\mathbf{\Upsilon}^{\prime}]_{21,-}=\ee^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}[\mathbf{\Upsilon}^{-1}\mathbf{\Upsilon}^{\prime}]_{21,+}
. Using then (
3.35
), (
3.36
) and (
3.38
), we get

\left[\mathbf{\Upsilon}^{-1}\mathbf{\Upsilon}^{\prime}\right]_{21,-}=\mathrm{i}\ee^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\left[\Phi\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\Phi^{\prime}-\Phi^{\prime}\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\Phi\right].

Taking one
\mathsf{x}
-derivative and using (
3.37
), we get that the
\mathsf{x}
-derivative of the right-hand side of the above formula is given by
\mathrm{i}\ee^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\Phi^{2}
.
Using this relation in (
5.6
), we see that

-\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\left(\log\mathsf{L}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{(Bes)}}(\mathsf{s},\mathsf{x})\right)=\frac{\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}}{2\pi}\int_{\mathsf{s}}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\Phi(\xi\mid\mathsf{s}=u,\mathsf{x})^{2}\partial_{\mathsf{s}}\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}(\xi\mid\mathsf{s}=u)\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}u.

When obtaining this identity, we exchanged the derivatives with the integrals, a calculation which is justified by the bounds obtained in (
4.8
)–(ii) and the exponential decay of
\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}
. From the definition of
\mathsf{p}
in (
2.20
) it follows that this identity is equivalent to (
2.22
), concluding the proof of Theorem
2.7
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.8
.

Suppose now that
m=1
. In particular, from (
2.13
) we see
that

\sigma_{\mathbf{\Phi}}(\zeta)=1/(1+\ee^{-\mathsf{s}+\zeta})
.
By setting

(5.7)

\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathsf{s}\mathsf{x}}{2}&1\end{pmatrix}{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty}(\zeta+\mathsf{s}),

it then follows from RHP
3.11
, (
3.30
) and direct calculations that
\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty}
satisfies the following RHP.

RHP 5.3
.

Find a
2\times 2
matrix-valued function
\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty}:\mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty,-\mathsf{s}]\to\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2}
with the following properties.

(i)

\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty,-\mathsf{s}]
.

(ii)

The entries of
\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty,\pm}
are continuous along
(-\infty,-\mathsf{s})
and satisfy

(5.8)

\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty,+}(\zeta)=\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty,-}(\zeta)\begin{pmatrix}\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}&\frac{1}{1+\ee^{\zeta}}\\
0&\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\zeta<0.

(iii)

As
\zeta\to\infty
, we have

(5.9)

\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty}(\zeta)=\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty,1}}{\zeta}+\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{-2})\right)\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\ee^{\mathsf{x}\zeta^{1/2}\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\left(\mathbf{I}+(\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{+}}(\zeta)-\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\alpha}\chi_{\mathcal{S}_{-}}(\zeta))\mathbf{E}_{21}\right),

where

(5.10)

\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty,1}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathsf{s}\mathsf{x}}{2}&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{q}-\frac{\mathsf{s}}{4}&-\mathrm{i}\mathsf{p}\\
\mathrm{i}\mathsf{r}&-\mathsf{q}+\frac{\mathsf{s}}{4}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathsf{s}\mathsf{x}}{2}&1\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\mathsf{s}^{2}\mathsf{x}^{2}}{8}&-\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathsf{s}\mathsf{x}}{2}\\
-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{24}\mathsf{s}^{2}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{s}\mathsf{x}^{2}+3)&-\frac{\mathsf{s}^{2}\mathsf{x}^{2}}{8}\end{pmatrix}.

(iv)

As
\zeta\to-\mathsf{s}
, we have

(5.11)

\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty}(\zeta)=\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty,0}(\zeta)(\zeta+\mathsf{s})^{\alpha\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathsf{a}(\zeta+\mathsf{s})\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)(1+\ee^{\zeta})^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2},

where

(5.12)

\widehat{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\infty,0}(\zeta)=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
